- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Pachacuti, leader of The Inca (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Inca
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Warak'aq, Replacing Slinger (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Inti Maceman, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Building: Qullqa, Replacing Granary (Requires technology: Pottery)
        - Unique Improvement: Pata-Pata (Can only be built on Hill. +1 Food for every two adjacent tiles with a Mountain, a Farm, a Pata-Pata or a Manufactory. Farms get +1 Food per 2 adjacent Farms or Pata-Pata.); Requires technology: Masonry
        - Civilization Ability: Units can cross Mountains and ignore terrain costs when entering any Hill or Mountain. Cities, Roads, and Railroads may be built on Mountains. Mountains produce +1 Production, Gold, and Science when worked, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Pachacuti
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Lasers: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Particle Physics, Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      
      ## Policies
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Space Procurements (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Space Procurements: May invest in Spaceship parts with Gold. Build Spaceship Factories in half the usual time. +20% Science from Research Labs.
      - Media Culture (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Media Culture: +25% Tourism and +1 Happiness generated by cities with a Broadcast Tower. +20% Culture from Stadiums.
      - Universal Suffrage (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Universal Suffrage: A Golden Age begins, and Golden Ages last 50% longer. Cities produce +1 Happiness.
      - Containment (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Containment: Trade Routes to City-States generate +2 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per owned City-State Trade Route (up to +10). +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 2 City-States originally in the World.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Majesty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Specialists in Capital consume half the normal amount of Food.
        - * Palace Gardens built in Capital (+5 Food, +25% Great Person Rate, -2 Unhappiness from Urbanization, 1 Great Work of Writing slot, 1 Writer Slot).
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Statecraft (New Branch):
        - Statecraft improves your ability to benefit from Trade Routes, City-States, Spies, and the World Congress.
        - Adopting Statecraft grants:
        - * +1 of every Yield in Capital for every 20 Citizens in your Empire.
        - * +1 Gold in every City.
        - Each Statecraft policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Gold in every City, and +10% Influence from Diplomatic Missions.
        - Adopting all policies in Statecraft grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Palace of Westminster.
        - * Every World Congress Session, gain 5 Culture, Science, and Gold for each Delegate you control, scaling with Era.
        - * World Wonders require 1 less unlocked Policy for every 3 City-State alliances you maintain.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Diplomats with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 8
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10
        - Boldness: 10
        - WarBias: 10
        - HostileBias: 10
        - WarmongerHate: 5
        - NeutralBias: 6
        - FriendlyBias: 6
        - GuardedBias: 6
        - AfraidBias: 3
        - DiplomaticBalance: 6
        - Friendliness: 7
        - WorkWithWillingness: 9
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 8
        - Loyalty: 6
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 10
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 6
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 4
        - MinorCivWarBias: 8
        - DenounceWillingness: 10
        - Forgiveness: 2
        - Meanness: 10
        - Neediness: 3
        - Chattiness: 5
        - DeceptiveBias: 10
        - Rationale: No changes. Our warmongering persona remains optimal for total war. We are the aggressor pursuing domination, so low WarmongerHate (5) reflects our role. High competitiveness, boldness, and war bias ensure relentless pressure on Sweden, Austria, and Siam. High minor civ competitiveness helps us secure allied city-states like Dorestad. Low forgiveness and high meanness keep our enemies permanently hostile, which aligns with our goal of eliminating them. The AI should continue to denounce, refuse peace, and prioritize military actions.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Enlightenment (Policy)
        - Rationale: With 12 turns to next policy, Enlightenment provides an immediate free technology—massively accelerating our tech progression toward Stealth or Lasers. The +1 Happiness from Universities also helps our current unhappiness (47%). This complements our conquest focus by giving a tech edge to field superior units.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### China
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: China has been eliminated (Beijing captured). Remove the extreme negative modifiers to clean up diplomatic memory. No further interaction needed.
        - UpdatedTurn: 337
        
        ### Utica
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Utica (Austrian-allied city-state) is at 17% health and under our assault. Maintain maximum private hostility to ensure AI prioritizes capturing it this turn, eliminating a naval threat near our core cities (Goshute, Moson Kahni). Public neutral as we are already at war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 363
        
        ### Salem
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: City‑State Salem is at 25% health and must be captured this turn. Maintain maximum private hostility (-100) to direct all nearby forces to finish it off, eliminating a flank threat and freeing units for other fronts.
        - UpdatedTurn: 374
        
        ### The Shoshone
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: The Shoshone are now eliminated (all cities captured). Remove the extreme negative modifier to clean up diplomatic memory. No further interaction needed as they are no longer a player.
        - UpdatedTurn: 425
        
        ### Germany
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Germany is now eliminated (Cologne captured). Remove the extreme negative modifier to clean up diplomatic memory. No further interaction needed.
        - UpdatedTurn: 425
        
        ### Songhai
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 99
        - Rationale: Songhai remains a loyal vassal providing 9 crucial trade routes that supply gold, science, and culture to our core cities. Maintaining maximum private positive modifier ensures they stay loyal and continue assisting in our multi-front wars, especially as we focus on the northern and eastern fronts against Sweden and Siam.
        - UpdatedTurn: 428
        
        ### Siam
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Siam is a cultural victory contender (already 5 influential civs) and holds Sukhothai (needed capital). They are in a defensive pact with Sweden and Austria. Maximum private hostility ensures our naval and air forces focus on eliminating their fleet and preparing invasion of Sukhothai to cut their tourism output.
        - UpdatedTurn: 431
        
        ### Sweden
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Sweden is our top competitor with massive military (70k+) and holds Stockholm and Berlin (both needed capitals). They are pressing our northern front hard. Maintain maximum private hostility to ensure AI prioritizes Swedish targets, does not consider peace, and focuses all forces on counterattacking Swedish units and preparing nuclear strikes on Stockholm.
        - UpdatedTurn: 431
        
        ### Austria
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Austria's Vienna is a needed capital (74 defense). Our military zones show strong friendly dominance; we must capitalize before Sweden reinforces. Maximum private hostility directs all nearby forces (including naval) toward capturing Vienna, especially after atomic bomb strikes.
        - UpdatedTurn: 431
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: We are at a decisive phase of the war: we hold 5 capitals, need Vienna (74 defense), Stockholm (109), and Sukhothai (77). Our northern front is under heavy Swedish pressure (events show losses of fleet destroyers). We must prioritize atomic bomb production (Nuke 100) to crack high‑defense capitals, especially Vienna which is more immediately reachable. Increase CityDefense to 95 to hold vulnerable cities like Cologne/Frankfurt/Innsbruck. Gold flavor raised to 90 to combat negative GPT (-119). Infrastructure to 75 for better logistics. All other military flavors remain maxed to sustain the multi‑front offensive. Grand strategy stays Conquest.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 42
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 3 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Incan Sphere of Influence: Dorestad:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.

            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.

              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Monaco:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: The Inca:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Swedish Sphere of Influence: Ife:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: World Ideology: Freedom:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - Proposer: Siam
          - Enact: Austrian Sphere of Influence: Florence:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: Austria
          - Enact: Swedish Sphere of Influence: Shedet:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: Sweden

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Pachacuti, leader of The Inca (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Inca
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Warak'aq, Replacing Slinger (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Inti Maceman, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Building: Qullqa, Replacing Granary (Requires technology: Pottery)
        - Unique Improvement: Pata-Pata (Can only be built on Hill. +1 Food for every two adjacent tiles with a Mountain, a Farm, a Pata-Pata or a Manufactory. Farms get +1 Food per 2 adjacent Farms or Pata-Pata.); Requires technology: Masonry
        - Civilization Ability: Units can cross Mountains and ignore terrain costs when entering any Hill or Mountain. Cities, Roads, and Railroads may be built on Mountains. Mountains produce +1 Production, Gold, and Science when worked, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Pachacuti
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Lasers: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Particle Physics, Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      
      ## Policies
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Space Procurements (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Space Procurements: May invest in Spaceship parts with Gold. Build Spaceship Factories in half the usual time. +20% Science from Research Labs.
      - Media Culture (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Media Culture: +25% Tourism and +1 Happiness generated by cities with a Broadcast Tower. +20% Culture from Stadiums.
      - Universal Suffrage (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Universal Suffrage: A Golden Age begins, and Golden Ages last 50% longer. Cities produce +1 Happiness.
      - Containment (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Containment: Trade Routes to City-States generate +2 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per owned City-State Trade Route (up to +10). +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 2 City-States originally in the World.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Majesty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Specialists in Capital consume half the normal amount of Food.
        - * Palace Gardens built in Capital (+5 Food, +25% Great Person Rate, -2 Unhappiness from Urbanization, 1 Great Work of Writing slot, 1 Writer Slot).
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Statecraft (New Branch):
        - Statecraft improves your ability to benefit from Trade Routes, City-States, Spies, and the World Congress.
        - Adopting Statecraft grants:
        - * +1 of every Yield in Capital for every 20 Citizens in your Empire.
        - * +1 Gold in every City.
        - Each Statecraft policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Gold in every City, and +10% Influence from Diplomatic Missions.
        - Adopting all policies in Statecraft grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Palace of Westminster.
        - * Every World Congress Session, gain 5 Culture, Science, and Gold for each Delegate you control, scaling with Era.
        - * World Wonders require 1 less unlocked Policy for every 3 City-State alliances you maintain.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Diplomats with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        - Flavors:
          - UseNuke: 100
          - CityDefense: 50
          - Defense: 70
          - Spaceship: 30
      - rationale: We have functional atomic weapons ready and are facing heavily entrenched Swedish and Austrian forces blocking our path east toward Siam. Raising UseNuke is critical so the tactical AI actually employs nukes to shatter enemy troop concentrations and cripple hostile capitals like Stockholm, breaking the stalemate. I have reduced CityDefense and Defense slightly to shift more resources into breakthrough offenses, since turtling plays into Siam's imminent cultural clock. A modest Spaceship flavor hedges our bets if conquest cannot be completed before Siam finishes the Citizen Earth Protocol.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Lasers
        - Rationale: Lasers unlocks Nuclear Missiles and leads toward Particle Physics, Nuclear Fusion, and Globalization. This gives us both a stronger nuclear arsenal to match Sweden's stockpile and an accelerated path to advanced science technologies, keeping the science-victory option open if domination cannot be achieved before Siam's cultural win.
  - tokens:
    - inputTokens: 74419
    - reasoningTokens: 17017
    - outputTokens: 233
  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Victory Progress:**
            - Domination: We control 4 capitals (Moson Kahni, Beijing, Gao, Cusco) = 50%. Need 8 total. Sweden controls 2 (Stockholm, Berlin).
            - Science: Apollo completed by Sweden and us. No parts built yet.
            - Cultural: Siam is Influential over all 5 civs needed! Siam could win Cultural very soon. We are Influential over 2 civs.
            - Diplomatic: Sweden leads with 18 delegates (42%), we have 11 (26%). World Congress voting in 3 turns.
            - We're at war with Siam, Sweden, and Austria.
            
            **Key Threats:**
            1. **Siam Cultural Victory**: Siam already has Influential on all 5 civs needed (Austria, Sweden, Songhai, The Inca, The Shoshone). They just need to finish their ideology tenets and build the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder. Wait, looking again: Siam shows InfluentialCivs: 5 and PolicyPercentage: 100. This means they have already satisfied the cultural victory conditions except perhaps building the wonder. Actually wait - let me check. The cultural victory requires: "Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder." Siam already influences all 5 civs and has 100% Policy. If they haven't built the wonder yet, they might be building it soon. This is an IMMINENT threat.
            
            2. **Sweden**: Very strong military (92,950 vs our 72,558). They have Information Era techs, 70 technologies. They're at war with us. They have 18 delegates and could try for diplomatic victory.
            
            3. **Our Military**: We have 72,558 strength. Not the strongest but still significant.
            
            **Current Strategy:**
            - GrandStrategy: Conquest
            - Flavors heavily military focused (Offense 100, Defense 95, Mobilization 100, etc.)
            - Policy next: Enlightenment
            - Research: None set, currently researching Stealth (13 turns)
            
            **Situation Assessment:**
            We're in a very dangerous position. Siam is about to win Cultural victory. We need to stop them immediately. However, we're at war with Sweden and Austria, not Siam directly? Wait, yes we are at war with Siam:
            - Siam: "War (Our Score: 10%; Our War Weariness: 17%)"
            - Sweden: "War (Our Score: 2%; Our War Weariness: 18%)"
            - Austria: "War (Our Score: 24%; Our War Weariness: 17%)"
            
            Wait, looking at Siam's relationships, they are at war with Songhai, The Inca, and The Shoshone. Our war score vs Siam is 10% (positive), which means we're doing well.
            
            But Siam is far away (Distance: Far). And Siam has an enormous tourism output (2192/turn).
            
            Can we realistically stop Siam culturally? We're at 2097 tourism/turn. We're Familiar with Siam (44%, 248 turns to Influential). That's too far.
            
            If Siam is truly about to win Cultural via Citizen Earth Protocol, we may need to capture their capital or key cities to stop them. But they're far away.
            
            Alternatively, we could try to rush our own victory. But:
            - Domination: We need 4 more capitals. Sweden controls Stockholm and Berlin. Austria has Vienna. The Shoshone have... wait, The Shoshone are our vassal. Their original capital is Moson Kahni which we already control. So remaining original capitals: Stockholm, Berlin, Vienna, Sukhothai, and... let's see. The Shoshone's original was Moson Kahni (ours). Songhai's original is Gao (ours). China's original was Beijing (ours). Germany's original... wait, Germany was defeated by Sweden? Actually Sweden controls Berlin. So capitals controlled:
              - Stockholm (Sweden)
              - Berlin (Sweden conquered Germany?)
              - Vienna (Austria)
              - Sukhothai (Siam)
              - And ours: Cusco, Beijing (China), Gao (Songhai), Moson Kahni (Shoshone)
              Total 8 needed, we have 4.
            
            Wait, actually Domination victory says "CapitalsNeeded: 8". There must be 8 original capitals total in the game. Players are: Siam, Shoshone, Sweden, Austria, Songhai, Inca = 6 major civs. But there are also defeated civs: China, Germany. That makes 8 total. So we need all 8. We have: Cusco, Beijing (China), Gao (Songhai), Moson Kahni (Shoshone). Need: Stockholm (Sweden), Berlin (Germany - held by Sweden), Vienna (Austria), Sukhothai (Siam).
            
            Wait, Germany is defeated but their capital Berlin is controlled by Sweden. Does controlling Berlin count? Yes, the victory says "Control or vassalize all original capitals." So we need to control/vassalize all 8 original capitals.
            
            Science: Both Sweden and us have Apollo. Sweden is slightly ahead in tech (70 vs our 69, but same era). 
            
            Diplomatic: We're far behind in delegates.
            
            **The most pressing issue: Stop Siam's cultural victory.**
            If Siam builds Citizen Earth Protocol, they win. We need to either:
            1. Capture Sukhothai or other Siam cities to reduce their culture/tourism output or stop the wonder.
            2. Win faster via another route.
            
            But Siam is far away. Do we have any way to reach them? Our territory extends to include Chinese cities, which are on the eastern side. Siam is further east. Distance: Far.
            
            Actually looking at the map coordinates:
            - Our easternmost cities: Nanjing (13, 11), Xian (12, 19), Shanghai (8, 10), Beijing (11, 6)
            - Siam cities: Sukhothai (66, 22), Si Satchanalai (64, 17), etc.
            That's very far - around 50+ tiles. We would need air power or ships.
            
            Wait, do we have ships that can reach? We have fleets, but range might be an issue. Actually this is a Vox Populi huge map essentially.
            
            Alternatively, since Siam is at war with us and we have a positive war score (10%), maybe our military IS engaging them somehow? But I don't see any tactical zones with Siam units. The military report only shows zones near Sweden, Austria, and our homeland. No Siam units visible. This suggests we don't have visibility or contact with Siam's main forces.
            
            If we cannot reach Siam quickly enough, we might lose to their cultural victory.
            
            But wait - World Congress is voting in 3 turns. There's a proposal to enact World Ideology: Freedom (proposed by Siam). If this passes, does it trigger diplomatic victory conditions? "Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed." But the UN hasn't been constructed yet, I think. Actually, the DiplomaticVictory section says "Status: World Congress - Voting in 3 turns". The UN needs to be built first.
            
            Looking at proposals: Siam proposed World Ideology Freedom. Austria proposed Sphere of Influence: Florence. Sweden proposed Sphere of Influence: Shedet. Also active resolutions include Swedish Sphere of Influence: Ife, and Incan Sphere of Influence: Dorestad (was this enacted? It says EnactedOn: -1, which means not enacted yet? Or -1 means active? Actually "EnactedOn: -1" might mean it's currently proposed/active. Wait, the section says "ActiveResolutions" - these are already enacted. Let me look again.
            
            "ActiveResolutions:
            - Incan Sphere of Influence: Dorestad... EnactedOn: -1"
            This might mean it was never enacted? Or -1 is a special value? Looking further:
            - Swedish Sphere of Influence: Ife, EnactedOn: -1
            Wait, these might be proposed resolutions, not active. But the header says ActiveResolutions. That's confusing. Actually below it says:
            "Proposals:
            - Enact: World Ideology: Freedom
            - Enact: Austrian Sphere of Influence: Florence
            - Enact: Swedish Sphere of Influence: Shedet"
            
            So the ActiveResolutions are already passed laws. The EnactedOn: -1 might just be a display bug or indicate the turn they were enacted. The key ones:
            - Cultural Heritage Sites (active) - helps everyone
            - Passport System (+50% tourism modifier for all) - this actually HELPS Siam!
            - Historical Landmarks
            - Open Door: Monaco
            - Sanction: The Inca (this hurts us! No trade routes/deals with us. But looking at our trades, we still have trade routes.)
            - Global Peace Accords (raised maintenance, higher warmonger penalties)
            - Swedish Sphere of Influence: Ife (active? Or proposed?)
            Wait the proposals section lists three new proposals. The Swedish Sphere of Influence: Shedet is a new proposal. Ife is already active? Actually in ActiveResolutions it says "Swedish Sphere of Influence: Ife" and "Incan Sphere of Influence: Dorestad". So those are already active! That means Sweden permanently allied Ife, and we permanently allied Dorestad. Good for us.
            
            Also "Open Door: Monaco" is active - can't ally Monaco. And "Sanction: The Inca" is active! This is bad.
            
            **Immediate Strategic Decisions Needed:**
